Treprostinil: (Moderate) Calcium-channel blockers can have additive hypotensive effects with other antihypertensive agents. This additive effect can be desirable, but the patient should be monitored carefully and the dosage should be adjusted based on clinical response.
Triamcinolone: (Moderate) Additive hypokalemia may occur when indapamide is coadministered with other drugs with a significant risk of hypokalemia such as systemic corticosteroids. Coadminister with caution and careful monitoring.
Triamterene: (Moderate) Monitor serum potassium concentrations in patients receiving angiotensin II receptor antagonists concomitantly with triamterene. Concomitant use may result in hyperkalemia. Triazolam: (Moderate) Monitor for signs of triazolam toxicity during coadministration with amlodipine and consider appropriate dose reduction of triazolam if clinically indicated. Coadministration may increase triazolam exposure. Triazolam is a sensitive CYP3A substrate and amlodipine is a weak CYP3A inhibitor.
Trifluoperazine: (Moderate) Indapamide may cause electrolyte disturbances, which may increase the potential for proarrhythmic effects of selected phenothiazines.
Trimethoprim: (Moderate) Monitor for hyperkalemia if concomitant use of an angiotensin II receptor antagonist and trimethoprim is necessary. Avoid concomitant use and consider alternative antibiotic therapy in patients with additional risk factors for hyperkalemia, including patients older than 65 years, those with underlying disorders of potassium metabolism, renal insufficiency, or those requiring high doses of trimethoprim. Amongst patients older than 65 years, concomitant use has been associated with a 2- to 7-fold increased risk of significant hyperkalemia compared to other antibiotics. Trimethoprim has a potassium-sparing effect on the distal nephron and may induce hyperkalemia, especially in those with pre-existing risk factors.

Tucatinib is a strong CYP3A inhibitor and amlodipine is a CYP3A substrate. Coadministration with a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor in elderly hypertensive patients increased systemic exposure to amlodipine by 60%. Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors may increase the plasma concentrations of amlodipine to a greater extent.

Vecuronium: (Moderate) Concomitant use of neuromuscular blockers and calcium-channel blockers may prolong neuromuscular blockade. (Moderate) Concomitant use of neuromuscular blockers and indapamide may prolong neuromuscular blockade, possibly due to hypokalemia or alterations in potassium concentrations across the end-plate membrane.
Vemurafenib: (Moderate) Vemurafenib is an inducer of CYP3A4 and decreased plasma concentrations of drugs metabolized by this enzyme, such as amlodipine, could be expected with concurrent use. Use caution, and monitor therapeutic effects of amlodipine when coadministered with vemurafenib.
Venlafaxine: (Moderate) Monitor for signs and symptoms of hyponatremia during concomitant diuretic and serotonin norepinephrine reuptake inhibitor (SNRI) use; consider discontinuing the SNRI if symptomatic hyponatremia occurs and institute appropriate medical intervention. Concomitant use increases the risk for developing hyponatremia.
Verapamil: (Moderate) Monitor for symptoms of hypotension and edema if coadministration of amlodipine with verapamil is necessary; adjust the dose of amlodipine as clinically appropriate. Amlodipine is a CYP3A substrate and verapamil is a moderate CYP3A inhibitor. Coadministration with a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor in elderly hypertensive patients increased systemic exposure to amlodipine by 60%. However, coadministration with another moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor in healthy volunteers did not significantly change amlodipine exposure.
Verteporfin: (Moderate) Use caution if coadministration of verteporfin with calcium channel blockers is necessary due to the risk of increased photosensitivity. Verteporfin is a light-activated drug used in photodynamic therapy; all patients treated with verteporfin will be photosensitive. Concomitant use with calcium channel blockers could enhance the rate of verteporfin uptake by the vascular endothelium, resulting in enhanced photosensitivity.
Vinorelbine: (Moderate) Monitor for an earlier onset and/or increased severity of vinorelbine-related adverse reactions, including constipation and peripheral neuropathy, if coadministration with amlodipine is necessary. Vinorelbine is a CYP3A4 substrate and amlodipine is a weak CYP3A4 inhibitor.
Vitamin B Complex Supplements: (Moderate) Cutaneous vasodilation induced by niacin may become problematic if high-dose niacin is used concomitantly with other antihypertensive agents, especially calcium-channel blockers. This effect is of particular concern in the setting of acute myocardial infarction, unstable angina, or other acute hemodynamic compromise. (Moderate) Cutaneous vasodilation induced by niacin may become problematic if high-dose niacin is used concomitantly with other antihypertensive agents. This effect is of particular concern in the setting of acute myocardial infarction, unstable angina, or other acute hemodynamic compromise.
Vonoprazan; Amoxicillin; Clarithromycin: (Major) Avoid coadministration of clarithromycin and amlodipine, particularly in geriatric patients, due to an increased risk of hypotension and acute kidney injury. If the use of a macrolide antibiotic is necessary in a patient receiving amlodipine therapy, azithromycin is the preferred agent. If coadministration is unavoidable, monitor for symptoms of hypotension and edema; adjust the dose of amlodipine as clinically appropriate. Amlodipine is a CYP3A4 substrate and clarithromycin is a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor. A retrospective, case crossover study, found the risk of hospitalization due to hypotension or shock to be significantly increased in geriatric patients exposed to clarithromycin during concurrent calcium-channel blocker therapy (OR 3.7, 95% CI 2.3-6.1). Concurrent use of azithromycin was not associated with an increased risk of hypotension (OR 1.5, 95% CI 0.8-2.8).
Voriconazole: (Moderate) Monitor for symptoms of hypotension and edema if coadministration of amlodipine with voriconazole is necessary; adjust the dose of amlodipine as clinically appropriate. Voriconazole is a strong CYP3A inhibitor and amlodipine is a CYP3A substrate. Coadministration with a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor in elderly hypertensive patients increased systemic exposure to amlodipine by 60%. Strong CYP3A4 inhibitors may increase the plasma concentrations of amlodipine to a greater extent.
Vortioxetine: (Moderate) Patients receiving a diuretic during treatment with vortioxetine may be at greater risk of developing syndrome of inappropriate antidiuretic hormone secretion (SIADH). Clinically significant hyponatremia has been reported during therapy with vortioxetine. One case involving serum sodium levels lower than 110 mmol/l has occurred. Hyponatremia may be potentiated by agents which can cause sodium depletion such as diuretics. Discontinuation of vortioxetine should be considered in patients who develop symptomatic hyponatremia.
Voxelotor: (Moderate) Monitor for symptoms of hypotension and edema if coadministration of amlodipine with voxelotor is necessary; adjust the dose of amlodipine as clinically appropriate. Amlodipine is a CYP3A substrate; voxelotor is a moderate CYP3A inhibitor. Coadministration with a moderate CYP3A inhibitor in elderly hypertensive patients increased systemic exposure to amlodipine by 60%. However, coadministration with another moderate CYP3A inhibitor in healthy volunteers did not significantly change amlodipine exposure.
Warfarin: (Moderate) Closely monitor the INR if coadministration of warfarin with amlodipine is necessary as concurrent use may increase the exposure of warfarin leading to increased bleeding risk. Amlodipine is a weak CYP3A4 inhibitor and the R-enantiomer of warfarin is a CYP3A4 substrate. The S-enantiomer of warfarin exhibits 2 to 5 times more anticoagulant activity than the R-enantiomer, but the R-enantiomer generally has a slower clearance. (Minor) The coadministration of telmisartan and warfarin may lead to a decrease in the anticoagulation effects of warfarin.
Zafirlukast: (Minor) Zafirlukast is a CYP3A4 inhibitor which theoretically may decrease the hepatic metabolism of amlodipine, a CYP3A4 substrate.
Ziprasidone: (Minor) Ziprasidone is a moderate antagonist of alpha-1 receptors and may cause orthostatic hypotension with or without tachycardia, dizziness, or syncope. Additive hypotensive effects are possible if ziprasidone is used concurrently with antihypertensive agents. (Minor) Ziprasidone is a moderate antagonist of alpha-1 receptors and may cause orthostatic hypotension with or without tachycardia, dizziness, or syncope. Additive hypotensive effects are possible if ziprasidone is used concurrently with antihypertensive agents.

Adverse Reactions
  Headline
  Adverse Reactions
  Adverse Reactions
    Headline
    edema, hypotension, peripheral edema
    General Information
    Symptomatic hypotension may occur with telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide therapy. In a 4-week placebo controlled trial (n = 295), 3.5% and 5.1% of individuals receiving 10 mg telmisartan, 1.25 mg amlodipine, 0.625 mg indapamide and 20 mg telmisartan, 2.5 mg amlodipine, 1.25 mg indapamide experienced symptomatic hypotension, respectively. In another study (n = 2,242), symptomatic hypotension was reported in 5.9% of subjects treated with telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide compared to 4% of telmisartan; indapamide subjects, 1.8% of telmisartan; amlodipine subjects, and 1.4% of amlodipine; indapamide subjects. Hypotension leading to worsening angina and acute myocardial infarction may develop after starting or increase the dose of amlodipine, particularly in individuals with severe obstructive coronary artery disease. Edema, including facial and lower limb/peripheral edema, has occurred with postmarketing use of telmisartan.

    Headline
    hyperkalemia, hypokalemia, hypomagnesemia, hyponatremia
    General Information
    Hypokalemia and hyponatremia have been reported with telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide therapy. In a 4-week placebo controlled trial (n = 295), 3.5% and 0.8% of individuals receiving 10 mg telmisartan, 1.25 mg amlodipine, 0.625 mg indapamide and 20 mg telmisartan, 2.5 mg amlodipine, 1.25 mg indapamide experienced hyponatremia (sodium less than 135 mmol/L), respectively. Hypokalemia (potassium less than 3.5 mmol/L) occurred in 3.5% and 5.1% of individuals receiving 10 mg telmisartan, 1.25 mg amlodipine, 0.625 mg indapamide and 20 mg telmisartan, 2.5 mg amlodipine, 1.25 mg indapamide, respectively. In a second study, hyponatremia (sodium concentration less than 135 mmol/L) was observed in 7.3% of individuals treated with telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide compared to 6.9% of telmisartan; indapamide individuals, 3.2% of telmisartan; amlodipine individuals, and 3.6% of amlodipine; indapamide individuals. Hypokalemia (potassium concentration less than 3.5 mmol/L) was observed in 6.8% of individuals treated with telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide compared to 4.7% of telmisartan; indapamide individuals, 0% of telmisartan; amlodipine individuals, and 12.7% of amlodipine; indapamide individuals. Hyperkalemia may occur with medications that inhibit the renin angiotensin-aldosterone system, such as telmisartan. Risk of hyperkalemia is higher in individuals with heart failure or renal impairment. Thiazide-like diuretics, such as indapamide, may cause hyponatremia, hypomagnesemia, and hypokalemia. Indapamide may also alter serum glucose concentrations and alter insulin requirements. Monitor electrolytes and glucose periodically during telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide therapy.

    Headline

    Headline
    choroidal effusion, myopia, ocular pain
    General Information
    Indapamide, a sulfonamide-derivative, may cause an idiosyncratic reaction, resulting in acute angle-closure glaucoma and elevated intraocular pressure with or without a noticeable acute myopic shift and/or choroidal effusions. Symptoms may include acute onset of decreased visual acuity or ocular pain and typically occur within hours to weeks of drug initiation. Permanent visual field loss may occur if angle-closure glaucoma is untreated. Choroidal effusion, acute myopia, and angle-closure glaucoma have occurred with postmarketing use of indapamide. If ocular pain or a decrease in vision occurs, promptly discontinue telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide therapy. Consider prompt medical or surgical treatment if the intraocular pressure remains uncontrolled.

Description
  Text
  Telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide is combination of an angiotensin II receptor blocker (ARB), dihydropyridine calcium channel blocker, and thiazide-like diuretic, respectively. It is indicated for the treatment of hypertension. ARBs, calcium channel blockers and thiazide diuretics are considered a first-line treatment options for individuals with hypertension as monotherapy or as part of combination antihypertensive therapy. Telmisartan selectively and competitively blocks the AT-1 receptor, which mediates the vasopressor and aldosterone effects of angiotensin II. Since telmisartan does not inhibit angiotensin converting enzyme (ACE), it does not result in bradykinin accumulation, which is theorized to cause the cough and angioedema associated with ACE inhibitors. Amlodipine inhibits the influx of calcium ions into vascular smooth muscle and cardiac muscle. It is a potent vasodilator and has a long half-life allowing for once-daily dosing. Although the mechanism of action is unclear, indapamide appears to act principally on the distal convoluted tubules of the nephron to enhance the excretion of sodium, chloride, and water by inhibiting the transport of sodium ions. Telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide can cause fetal harm when administered during pregnancy; thus, it should be discontinued as soon as possible once pregnancy is detected.Telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide was approved by the FDA in 2025.

Mechanism Of Action
  Text
  Telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide target three separate mechanisms involved in blood pressure regulation.
Telmisartan: Telmisartan antagonizes angiotensin II (AG II) at the AT1 receptor subtype in tissues, such as smooth muscle and the adrenal gland. Two angiotensin II receptors, AT1 and AT2, have been identified. While telmisartan has about a 3,000-fold greater affinity for the AT1 subtype than the AT2 subtype, the AT2 subtype is not known to mediate cardiovascular homeostasis. Angiotensin II is the primary vasoactive hormone of the renin-angiotensin system and plays an important role in the pathophysiology of hypertension and congestive heart failure. Besides being a potent vasoconstrictor, the effects of angiotensin II include stimulation of the synthesis and release of aldosterone, cardiac stimulation, and renal reabsorption of sodium. By blocking the effects of angiotensin II, telmisartan decreases systemic vascular resistance without a marked change in heart rate. Circulating levels of both renin and angiotensin II rise in response to blockade of AT1 receptors and, subsequently, increased stimulation of the AT2 receptors by angiotensin II. Telmisartan does not inhibit angiotensin converting enzyme (ACE, kininase II) and, thus, does not inhibit the breakdown of bradykinin.  
Amlodipine: Amlodipine inhibits the influx of extracellular calcium across the cardiac muscle and vascular smooth muscle cell membranes. While serum calcium levels remain unchanged, intracellular concentrations decrease. The decrease in intracellular calcium inhibits the contractility of smooth muscle cells resulting in dilation of coronary and systemic arteries. This dilation results in decreased vascular resistance, increased coronary blood flow, and increased oxygen delivery to myocardial tissue. Myocardial oxygen consumption is also reduced. As with other dihydropyridine calcium channel blockers, amlodipine exerts its effects mainly on arteriolar vasculature. It has no significant effect on sinus node function or cardiac conduction, nor does it possess negative inotropic effects at clinical doses. Due to its gradual onset, reflex tachycardia does not occur, a side effect that is common with other peripheral vasodilators. Amlodipine therapy usually does not affect hemodynamic parameters in patients with normal ventricular function.  
Indapamide: Although the mechanism of action is not clear, indapamide, a thiazide-like diuretic, appears to act principally on the distal convoluted tubules of the nephron. The drug enhances the excretion of sodium, chloride, and water by inhibiting the transport of sodium ions across the renal tubule. The hypovolemic action of indapamide is believed to be responsible for the drug's beneficial cardiovascular effects. Decreased plasma and extracellular fluid volume, along with a decreased peripheral vascular resistance (secondary to loss of sodium, or to vascular autoregulatory feedback systems), act to lower blood pressure in hypertensive patients who are receiving indapamide. The drug may also produce calcium-channel blockade in smooth muscle cells, thereby causing arteriolar vasodilation.

Pharmacokinetics
  Route Specific Pharmacokinetics
    Name
    Oral Route
    Description
    Following administration of a single oral dose (40 mg telmisartan; 5 mg amlodipine; 2.5 mg indapamide) to healthy individuals in a fasted state, the AUC and Cmax of telmisartan was 750 nanograms per hour/mL and 64.7 nanograms/mL, respectively; the AUC and Cmax of amlodipine was 102,000 picograms per hour/mL and 2,850 picograms/mL, respectively; and the AUC and Cmax of indapamide was 2,170 nanograms per hour/mL and 144 nanograms/mL, respectively. The peak plasma concentrations of telmisartan, amlodipine, and indapamide are achieved at approximately 7.5 hours, 1.8 hours, and 0.9 hours, respectively. Administration of 40 mg telmisartan; 5 mg amlodipine; 2.5 mg indapamide to healthy individuals after a high-fat high calorie breakfast decreased the telmisartan Cmax by 41%, while the AUC remained unchanged. Additionally, the Cmax and AUC of amlodipine and indapamide remained unchanged when compared to administration under fasting conditions. Under fed conditions, the Tmax of telmisartan, amlodipine, and indapamide was delayed by approximately 2 hours, 1 hour, and 1.5 hours, respectively.
  Special Populations
    Name
    Hepatic Impairment
    Description
    Telmisartan: The elimination of telmisartan may be reduced by hepatic impairment. In individuals with hepatic insufficiency, telmisartan plasma concentrations are increased, and the absolute bioavailability approaches 100%.
Amlodipine: In individuals with hepatic impairment, amlodipine clearance is decreased, resulting in a 40% to 60% increase in AUC.
Indapamide: The effect of hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of indapamide is unknown.
    Name
    Renal Impairment
    Description
    Telmisartan: The elimination of telmisartan is not altered by renal impairment, and it is not removed from blood by hemodialysis.
Amlodipine: Renal impairment does not significantly influence the pharmacokinetics of amlodipine.
Indapamide: The effects of renal impairment on the pharmacokinetics of indapamide are unknown.
    Name
    Geriatric
    Description
    Telmisartan: There are no differences in the pharmacokinetics of telmisartan between the elderly and those less than 65 years of age.
Amlodipine: Elderly individuals have a reduced amlodipine clearance, resulting in a 40% to 60% increase in AUC.
Indapamide: The pharmacokinetics of indapamide in geriatric individuals is unknown.
    Name
    Gender Differences
    Description
    Plasma telmisartan concentrations are generally 2 to 3 times higher in females than in males; however, these differences have not resulted in significant increases in blood pressure response or the incidence of orthostatic hypotension; no dosage adjustment is needed.
  Text
  Telmisartan; amlodipine; indapamide is administered orally.
Telmisartan: Telmisartan is highly bound to plasma proteins (more than 99.5%), mainly albumin and alpha-1-acid glycoprotein. Plasma protein binding remains constant over the concentration range achieved with recommended doses. The volume of distribution is approximately 500 L, indicating additional tissue binding. Telmisartan undergoes metabolism via conjugation to form a pharmacologically inactive acylglucuronide. After a single telmisartan dose, the glucuronide represents approximately 11% of the measured radioactivity in plasma. The cytochrome P450 isoenzymes are not involved in telmisartan metabolism. Following oral administration of radioactively labeled telmisartan, more than 97% of the administered dose is eliminated unchanged in the feces via biliary excretion; only minute amounts are found in the urine (0.49% of total radioactivity, respectively). Total plasma clearance of telmisartan is more than 800 mL/min and the elimination half-life is 24 hours. The terminal half-life and total clearance appear to be independent of the dose.
 
Amlodipine: Amlodipine is approximately 93% bound to plasma proteins and has an apparent volume of distribution of 21 L/kg. It undergoes extensive hepatic metabolism (about 90%) to inactive metabolites with 10% of the parent compound and 60% of the metabolites excreted in the urine. Plasma elimination of amlodipine is biphasic with a terminal elimination half-life of about 30 to 50 hours. Steady state plasma levels are reached after 7 to 8 days of consecutive daily dosing.
 
Indapamide: Indapamide is 71% to 79% reversibly bound to plasma proteins and has a volume of distribution of 25 L. It is preferentially and reversibly taken up by erythrocytes, resulting in a whole blood to plasma ratio of 6:1 at the time of peak concentration and 3.5:1 at 8 hours. Indapamide undergoes extensive hepatic metabolism, with only about 7% of the total dose recovered in the urine as unchanged drug within 48 hours of administration. The urinary elimination of radioactively-labeled indapamide and metabolites is biphasic with a terminal half-life of excretion of total radioactivity of 26 hours. Following administration of a single indapamide dose, at least 70% of the dose is eliminated by the kidneys and 23% by the gastrointestinal tract, probably including the biliary route. The half-life of indapamide in whole blood is approximately 14 hours.
 
Affected cytochrome P450 isoenzymes and drug transporters: CYP2C19, CYP3A
Telmisartan is not metabolized by the cytochrome P450 system and has no effects in vitro on cytochrome P450 enzymes, except for some inhibition of CYP2C19. Therefore, it is not expected to interact with drugs metabolized by cytochrome P450 enzymes, except for possible inhibition of drugs metabolized by CYP2C19. Amlodipine is a substrate and weak inhibitor of CYP3A. Strong CYP3A inhibitors may increase plasma concentrations of amlodipine. In addition, amlodipine may increase systemic exposure of other CYP3A substrates.

Dosage Adjustment Guidelines
  Hepatic Impairment
  Initiate therapy at the lowest dose (10 mg telmisartan; 1.25 mg amlodipine; 0.625 mg indapamide) and slowly titrate dose in individuals with hepatic impairment. Use indapamide with caution in individuals with hepatic disease since it may precipitate hepatic coma due to fluid shifts.
  Renal Impairment
  Specific guidelines for dosage adjustments in renal impairment are not available; it appears that no dosage adjustments are needed.:
 
Intermittent hemodialysis:
Telmisartan is not significantly removed by hemodialysis.
